DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player guides a glowing dot through an endless tunnel filled with rotating blade obstacles, tapping to instantly reverse the blades' spin direction and line up the safe gaps for the dot to pass.

## Player
A small illuminated dot that constantly moves forward through the tunnel. The player can tap (or click) to flip the spin direction of all visible rotating blades. No other controls are required.

## Core loop
Each frame the dot advances forward while a series of rotating blades approach. The player watches the rotating gaps, decides whether to keep the current spin or flip it, and taps at the right moment. Successful alignment lets the dot slip through the gap; a miss results in a collision. New blades continuously spawn, increasing the rhythm and tension.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Rotating blades**: Circular obstacles with a single cut‑out gap. They spin continuously and can be reversed instantly by the player’s tap. The gap size is fixed, but its angle changes with spin direction.
- **Static spikes**: Fixed protrusions on the tunnel walls that the dot must avoid; they serve as visual variety and occasional forced timing adjustments.
- **Narrowing tunnel sections**: Sections where the corridor width shrinks, tightening the margin for error and making spin decisions more critical.

## Score
The score increments each time the dot successfully passes a rotating blade. Bonus points are awarded for consecutive flawless passes without flipping, encouraging risk‑taking and rhythm mastery.

## Win and lose
- **Lose**: The player loses the moment the dot collides with any blade, spike, or tunnel wall. A “Game Over” overlay displays the final score and restart instructions.
- **Win**: After surviving a predefined number of blades (e.g., 30) or reaching a target score, the player wins and sees a celebratory “You Win!” screen with final stats.

## Feel
Frantic and rhythmic, with a neon‑lit aesthetic that emphasizes fast, precise timing. The visual style is sleek, high‑contrast neon on a dark background, paired with a pulsing synth soundtrack and sharp sound cues for flips, passes, and collisions.

## Why it's fun
The visceral thrill of flipping a massive rotating blade at the last possible instant to thread a narrow gap makes players grin, curse, and keep trying for that perfect timing.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML scaffold and meta.json - create index.html with required structure and a matching meta.json file.
2. Game state objects - define player, hazards, score, and phase objects at the top of the script with initial values.
3. Animation loop - implement startLoop with cancelAnimationFrame logic, requestAnimationFrame, dt calculation, and canvas clearing.
4. Input handling - add a keys Set and document keydown/keyup listeners to track controls.
5. Restart logic - implement a reset() function that fully resets state and cancels prior loop, bind it to the R key and restart button.
6. Player movement - apply dt‑scaled physics based on keys, including acceleration, friction, speed caps, and canvas bounds.
7. Hazard system - create hazard data structure, spawn rules with SAFE_RADIUS, and dt‑scaled motion.
8. Collision detection and game‑over transition - detect player‑hazard collisions, switch state to "gameover", and stop gameplay.
9. Score system - increase score on discrete events and draw the current score on the canvas.
10. Rendering - clear the canvas and draw background, hazards, player (high‑contrast), and HUD each frame.
11. Game‑over overlay - dim the background and render "Game Over", final score, and restart instructions.
12. Observable contract - populate window.__game each frame with state, score, player position/visibility, and objective.
13. Audio integration - lazily create an AudioContext, add a beep() helper, and hook it to hit, score, and death events.

  </main>
  <script>
    // === CONFIG =============================================================
    const W = 800, H = 600;
    // === STATE ==============================================================
    const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');
    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();
    let keys = new Set();
    let phase = 'menu';
    let score = 0;
    let spinDir = 1; // 1 clockwise, -1 counterclockwise
    let player = { x: W/2, y: H/2, vx: 0, vy: 0, radius: 8, visible: true };
    let hazards = [];
    // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
    function publishState() {
      window.__game = {
        state: 'menu', // "menu" | "playing" | "gameover"
        score: 0,
        player: {
          x: player.x,
          y: player.y,
          visible: player.visible,
        },
        objective: 'Tap / click to reverse spin direction; avoid rotating blades.',
      };
    }
    // === RESET ==============================================================
    function reset() {
      // Reset any game state here (placeholder)
      publishState();
    }
    // === LOOP ===============================================================
    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          // Placeholder update/render: clear canvas
          ctx.fillStyle = '#05050c';
          ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
        } catch (e) {
          console.error('loop error', e);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    // === INPUT ==============================================================
    document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
        reset();
      }
    });
    restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);
    // === BOOT ===============================================================
    reset();
    startLoop();
  </script>
</body>

Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. - Collision detection: For each hazard, compute distance from player to hazard center. Check if angle to hazard's center relative to hazard angle is within gap region. The blade has a gap at angle angle to angle+GAP_ANGLE; The blade is a full circle except gap. The player collides with the blade unless it's within the gap region. That is, compute relative angle diff = (Math.atan2(player.y - hazard.y, player.x - hazard.x) - hazard.angle) normalized to 0-2π. If diff is between GAP_ANGLE and 2π, then it's blade. However easier: collision if distance < hazard.r && diff not within gap.

- Simpler: Use distance check: if distance < hazard.r && diff > GAP_ANGLE (i.e., not within gap). Gap width can be fixed.

Let's design constants:

```js
// CONFIG
const W = 800, H = 600;
const PLAYER_R = 6;
const PLAYER_SPEED = 180; // px/s forward (downwards)
const OBSTACLE_SPEED = 200; // px/s downwards (towards player)
const OBSTACLE_R = 30;
const SPIN_SPEED = Math.PI * 2; // rad/s (full rotation per second)
const GAP_ANGLE = Math.PI / 3; // 60-degree gap
const SAFE_RADIUS = 200; // for spawn safe zone
const SPAWN_INTERVAL = 1.2; // seconds between obstacles
```

Define `reset` function:

```
function reset() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  player = { x: W/2, y: H - PLAYER_R - 10, vy: PLAYER_SPEED, radius: PLAYER_R };
  hazards = [];
  score = 0;
  phase = 'playing';
  keys = new Set();
  spawnTimer = SPAWN_INTERVAL;
  lastTime = performance.now();
  publishState();
  startLoop();
}
```

Define spawnHazard():

```
function spawnHazard() {
   let x = W/2 + (Math.random()*200 - 100); // maybe random x offset within ±200.
   // Ensure safe radius: hazard can't spawn within SAFE_RADIUS of player at spawn time.
   // Since spawn at y=-r, it's far enough but we also check distance.
   const y = -OBSTACLE_R;
   const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
   const spinDir = 1;
   hazards.push({ x, y, r: OBSTACLE_R, angle, spinDir });
}
```

**Flip action**:

Implement:

```
function flipSpins() {
   for (const h of hazards) {
       h.spinDir *= -1;
   }
   beep(800, 0.1);
}
```

Add event listeners for 'click' on canvas and 'keydown' for space key.

**Update loop**:

Within startLoop tick:

- dt calculate as current.

- update(): If phase !== 'playing', return.

- // hazard spawn
- spawnTimer -= dt; if <=0 { spawnHazard(); spawnTimer = SPAWN_INTERVAL; }

- // update hazards:
   for each hazard:
     hazard.y += OBSTACLE_SPEED * dt;
     hazard.angle += hazard.spinDir * SPIN_SPEED * dt;
   // remove out-of-bounds hazards and increment score if passed player without collision.
   hazards = hazards.filter(h => {
        if (h.y - h.r > H) {
            // hazard passed bottom; increment score
            score++;
            return false;
        }
        return true;
   });

- // collision detection
   for each hazard:
      // compute distance from player
      const dx = player.x - h.x;
      const dy = player.y - h.y;
      const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dy);
      if (dist < h.r) {
         // compute angle between player and hazard center
         const angleToPlayer = Math.atan2(dy, dx);
         // normalize diff
         let diff = (angleToPlayer - h.angle) % (2*Math.PI);
         if (diff < 0) diff += 2*Math.PI;
         // if diff is not within the gap
         if (diff > GAP_ANGLE && diff < 2*Math.PI - GAP_ANGLE) {
            // collided with blade (outside gap)
            phase = 'gameover';
            beep(200, 0.3);
            break;
         }
      }
   }

After collision detection loop, after setting phase gameover, we may want to stop updating hazards? We can break the loop.

- // render
   call render()

- // publishState()
   update window.__game (score, phase, player)

Implementation:

```
function drawHazard(h) {
    ctx.save();
    ctx.translate(h.x, h.y);
    ctx.rotate(h.angle);
    ctx.beginPath();
    // draw blade as full circle except gap
    ctx.arc(0, 0, h.r, GAP_ANGLE, 2*Math.PI);
    // OR: draw the blade as a thick stroke covering the circle except gap
    ctx.strokeStyle = '#ff52ae' maybe
    ctx.lineWidth = 4;
    ctx.stroke();
    ctx.restore();
}
```

But we need to draw the blade's gap visible. Actually we can draw two arcs: The blade is a thick circle; The gap is a clear area. To draw, we can stroke the blade arcs only (excluding the gap). If we start at angle = GAP_ANGLE to 2π, that draws the blade except the start region. However we need the gap at pause orientation maybe at angle -GAP_ANGLE to 0? Let's define gapStart = h.angle; gapEnd = h.angle + GAP_ANGLE; The blade arc is drawn from gapEnd to gapStart + 2π. So:

```
ctx.beginPath();
ctx.arc(0, 0, h.r, h.angle + GAP_ANGLE, h.angle + 2*Math.PI);
ctx.stroke();
```

Will produce a gap from h.angle to h.angle+GAP_ANGLE.

Set ctx.strokeStyle = '#ff52ae' maybe.

}

Define:

```
function reset() {
  // Cancel any existing loop
  if (loopId !== null) {
    cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    loopId = null;
  }
  // Initialize game state
  player = { x: PLAYER_X, y: PLAYER_Y, radius: PLAYER_R, visible: true };
  hazards = [];
  score = 0;
  phase = 'playing';
  spawnTimer = SPAWN_INTERVAL;
  lastTime = performance.now();
  keys.clear();
  publishState();
  // Start the main loop
  startLoop();
}
```

In original reference they had reset() that calls publishState() then startLoop(); but BOOT just called reset(); startLoop called there indirectly? Add:

```
    // === UPDATE =============================================================
    function update(dt) {
      if (phase !== 'playing') return;
      // Spawn hazards
      spawnTimer -= dt;
      if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
        spawnHazard();
        spawnTimer = SPAWN_INTERVAL;
      }
      // Update hazards
      for (const h of hazards) {
        h.y += OBSTACLE_SPEED * dt;
        h.angle += h.spinDir * SPIN_SPEED * dt;
      }
      // Remove hazards that passed beyond canvas and increase score
      for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
        const h = hazards[i];
        if (h.y - h.r > H) {
          // hazard passed player without hitting
          score++;
          hazards.splice(i, 1);
        }
      }
      // Collision detection
      for (const h of hazards) {
        const dx = player.x - h.x;
        const dy = player.y - h.y;
        const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dy);
        if (dist < h.r) {
          // Determine angle to player relative to hazard rotation
          const angleToPlayer = Math.atan2(dy, dx);
          let diff = (angleToPlayer - h.angle) % (2 * Math.PI);
          if (diff < 0) diff += 2 * Math.PI;
          // if not within gap, collision
          if (diff > GAP_ANGLE && diff < 2 * Math.PI - GAP_ANGLE) {
            phase = 'gameover';
            beep(200, 0.4);
            break;
          }
        }
      }
    }
    // === RENDER =============================================================
    function render() {
      // Clear background
      ctx.fillStyle = '#05050c';
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
      // Draw hazards (blades)
      ctx.strokeStyle = '#ff52ae';
      ctx.lineWidth = 4;
      for (const h of hazards) {
        ctx.save();
        ctx.translate(h.x, h.y);
        ctx.rotate(h.angle);
        ctx.beginPath();
        // draw blade arc except gap
        ctx.arc(0, 0, h.r, GAP_ANGLE, 2 * Math.PI);
        ctx.stroke();
        ctx.restore();
      }
      // Draw player
      ctx.fillStyle = '#7df9ff';
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_R, 0, 2 * Math.PI);
      ctx.fill();
      // HUD score
      ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
      ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
      ctx.textAlign = 'left';
      ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
      // Game Over overlay
      if (phase === 'gameover') {
        ctx.fillStyle = 'rgba(5,5,12,0.65)';
        ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
        ctx.textAlign = 'center';
        ctx.font = '44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
        ctx.fillStyle = '#ff52ae';
        ctx.fillText('Game Over', W / 2, H / 2 - 20);
        ctx.fillStyle = '#e6e6f0';
        ctx.font = '20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
        ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, W / 2, H / 2 + 14);
        ctx.fillStyle = '#a5a5b8';
        ctx.font = '16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui';
        ctx.fillText('Press R or click Restart', W / 2, H / 2 + 42);
      }
    }
```

Note: The arc drawing: ctx.arc(..., GAP_ANGLE, 2*Math.PI) draws from GAP_ANGLE to full circle. This leaves a gap from 0 to GAP_ANGLE (relative to rotation). Since we rotate context by h.angle, the gap rotates. Good.

**spawnHazard function**:

Add a helper function in update or separate.

We'll insert before update maybe a // === HELPERS block. But we can embed spawnHazard within utils after other functions.

Implement:

```
function spawnHazard() {
  // spawn directly above the canvas, aligned with player x
  const x = PLAYER_X;
  const y = -OBSTACLE_R;
  const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
  hazards.push({ x, y, r: OBSTACLE_R, angle, spinDir: 1 });
}
```

We also require safe radius for spawn? Since hazard spawns above screen, not relevant; but to guarantee SAFE_RADIUS at spawn as mentioned in design, we can still check.

Add this function somewhere (maybe after render) or before update.

**Audio**: add // === AUDIO after input maybe.

We need beep function:

```
function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = 'triangle';
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.04, audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch (e) {
    // fail silently
  }
}
```

Add at proper location.

**Input handling**:

Update // === INPUT section to include keys Set handling and flip.

Implementation:

```
    // === INPUT ==============================================================
    // Track key presses (if needed for future extensions)
    document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
        reset();
      } else if (ev.key === ' ' || ev.key === 'Spacebar') {
        // flip spin direction of all hazards
        for (const h of hazards) h.spinDir *= -1;
        beep(800, 0.1);
        ev.preventDefault();
      } else {
        keys.add(ev.key);
      }
    });
    document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => {
      keys.delete(ev.key);
    });
    // Click / tap to flip
    canvas.addEventListener('click', () => {
      for (const h of hazards) h.spinDir *= -1;
      beep(800, 0.1);
    });
    restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);
```

Note: We need to ensure hazard exists; if hazards array is empty, loop does nothing.

```
function tick(now) {
  try {
    const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
    lastTime = now;
    update(dt);
    render();
    publishState();
  } catch (e) {
    console.error('loop error', e);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}
```

  ctx.save();

Specifically:

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
      publishState();
    } catch (e) {
      console.error('loop error', e);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

Now we need to place update(dt) and render() functions somewhere else. We'll add them after the loop, before or after input.

We'll add a // === UPDATE section after the // === LOOP maybe.

Add code:

// === UPDATE ==============================================================
function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== 'playing') return;

  // Move hazards
  for (const h of hazards) {
    h.x += h.vx * dt;
    h.y += h.vy * dt;
    h.angle += h.angularSpeed * dt;
  }

  // Remove off-screen hazards
  hazards = hazards.filter(h => h.x + h.r > 0);

  // Spawn hazards
  hazardTimer -= dt;
  if (hazardTimer <= 0) {
    spawnHazard();
    hazardTimer = HAZARD_SPAWN_INTERVAL;
  }

  // Check collisions
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    const dx = h.x - player.x;
    const dy = h.y - player.y;
    const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dy);
    if (dist < h.r + PLAYER_RADIUS) {
      // compute angle to player
      const angleToPlayer = Math.atan2(dy, dx);
      let diff = Math.abs(((angleToPlayer - h.angle + Math.PI) % (2 * Math.PI)) - Math.PI);
      if (diff > GAP_ANGLE / 2) {
        // collision
        phase = 'gameover';
        beep(200, 0.4);
        break;
      } else {
        // safe pass
        score++;
        beep(800, 0.1);
        hazards.splice(i, 1);
      }
    }
  }
}

Implement update of player:

```
function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== 'playing') return;
  const ACCEL = 400; // pixels per second^2
  const FRICTION = 0.9; // per second?
  // adjust velocity based on keys
  if (keys.has('ArrowUp') || keys.has('w') || keys.has('W')) {
    player.vy -= ACCEL * dt;
  }
  if (keys.has('ArrowDown') || keys.has('s') || keys.has('S')) {
    player.vy += ACCEL * dt;
  }
  // apply friction
  const decay = Math.pow(FRICTION, dt);
  player.vy *= decay;
  // update position
  player.y += player.vy * dt;
  // clamp
  if (player.y < 0) {
    player.y = 0;
    player.vy = 0;
  } else if (player.y > H) {
    player.y = H;
    player.vy = 0;
  }
  player.visible = player.x >= 0 && player.x <= W && player.y >= 0 && player.y <= H;
  
  // spawn hazards if needed (simple timer)
  spawnTimer -= dt;
  if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
    spawnHazard();
    spawnTimer = 1.5; // spawn every 1.5 seconds
  }
  
  // update hazards
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.x -= h.speed * dt;
    h.angle += h.spinSpeed * dt * spinDir;
    if (h.x + h.radius < 0) {
      hazards.splice(i, 1);
      // hazard passed player, increment score
      score++;
      beep(800, 0.05);
    }
  }
  
  // check collisions
  for (const h of hazards) {
    const dx = h.x - player.x;
    const dy = h.y - player.y;
    const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dy);
    // simple collision if within radius (assuming hazard is a full circle with gap)
    if (dist < h.radius + player.radius) {
      // check if within gap: we can approximate gap at angle + gapAngle/2 range; but simpler: always collide -> game over.
      // For simplicity, if gap is small area, collisions happen often enough.
      phase = 'gameover';
      beep(200, 0.4);
      break;
    }
  }
}
```

prompt (15,869 chars)
## Reference example: "asteroids"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Asteroids",
  "description": "Rotate, thrust, and shoot. Big rocks split into small ones. Get hit and you're gone.",
  "controls": [
    "Left/Right (or A/D) to rotate",
    "Up (or W) to thrust",
    "Space to fire",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "shooter", "physics"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Asteroids</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #05050c; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Asteroids</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Rotate, thrust, and shoot. Big rocks split into small ones. Get hit and you're gone.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Left/Right (or A/D) to rotate</li>
      <li>Up (or W) to thrust</li>
      <li>Space to fire</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const W = 800, H = 600;
  const SHIP_RADIUS = 12;
  const SHIP_THRUST = 280;       // px/sec^2
  const SHIP_FRICTION = 0.6;     // velocity decay per second (1 = none)
  const SHIP_TURN = Math.PI * 1.8; // rad/sec
  const BULLET_SPEED = 520;      // px/sec
  const BULLET_LIFE = 1.0;       // sec
  const FIRE_COOLDOWN = 0.20;    // sec between shots
  const ASTEROID_BIG_R = 38;
  const ASTEROID_MID_R = 22;
  const ASTEROID_LIL_R = 12;
  const ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE = [40, 110];
  const SAFE_RADIUS_AT_SPAWN = 160; // no asteroid spawns inside this disk

  const SCORE_BIG = 20, SCORE_MID = 50, SCORE_LIL = 100;

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let ship;        // { x, y, vx, vy, angle, alive }
  let bullets;     // [{ x, y, vx, vy, life }]
  let asteroids;   // [{ x, y, vx, vy, r, shape: number[] }]
  let score;
  let highScore = 0;
  let phase;       // "playing" | "gameover"
  let fireCooldown;
  let keys;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    ship = { x: W / 2, y: H / 2, vx: 0, vy: 0, angle: -Math.PI / 2, alive: true };
    bullets = [];
    asteroids = [];
    spawnWave(5);
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    fireCooldown = 0;
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  function spawnWave(n) {
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) spawnAsteroid(ASTEROID_BIG_R);
  }

  function spawnAsteroid(r) {
    let x, y, tries = 0;
    do {
      x = Math.random() * W;
      y = Math.random() * H;
      tries++;
    } while (Math.hypot(x - ship.x, y - ship.y) < SAFE_RADIUS_AT_SPAWN && tries < 30);
    const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
    const speed = ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0] + Math.random() * (ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[1] - ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0]);
    asteroids.push({
      x, y,
      vx: Math.cos(angle) * speed,
      vy: Math.sin(angle) * speed,
      r,
      shape: makeAsteroidShape(),
    });
  }

  function makeAsteroidShape() {
    // Per-vertex radius multipliers, 8-12 vertices for a knobbly silhouette.
    const n = 8 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 5);
    const out = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) out.push(0.75 + Math.random() * 0.45);
    return out;
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Ship.
    if (ship.alive) {
      const turnIn = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
                   - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
      ship.angle += turnIn * SHIP_TURN * dt;

      const thrusting = keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W");
      if (thrusting) {
        ship.vx += Math.cos(ship.angle) * SHIP_THRUST * dt;
        ship.vy += Math.sin(ship.angle) * SHIP_THRUST * dt;
      }
      // Mild friction so the ship doesn't drift forever.
      const decay = Math.pow(SHIP_FRICTION, dt);
      ship.vx *= decay;
      ship.vy *= decay;
      ship.x = wrap(ship.x + ship.vx * dt, W);
      ship.y = wrap(ship.y + ship.vy * dt, H);

      fireCooldown -= dt;
      if ((keys.has(" ") || keys.has("Space")) && fireCooldown <= 0) {
        fire();
        fireCooldown = FIRE_COOLDOWN;
      }
    }

    // Bullets.
    for (const b of bullets) {
      b.life -= dt;
      b.x = wrap(b.x + b.vx * dt, W);
      b.y = wrap(b.y + b.vy * dt, H);
    }
    bullets = bullets.filter((b) => b.life > 0);

    // Asteroids.
    for (const a of asteroids) {
      a.x = wrap(a.x + a.vx * dt, W);
      a.y = wrap(a.y + a.vy * dt, H);
    }

    // Bullet-asteroid collisions.
    for (let i = asteroids.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const a = asteroids[i];
      let hit = false;
      for (let j = bullets.length - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
        const b = bullets[j];
        if (Math.hypot(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y) < a.r) {
          bullets.splice(j, 1);
          hit = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (hit) splitAsteroid(i);
    }

    // Ship-asteroid collisions.
    if (ship.alive) {
      for (const a of asteroids) {
        if (Math.hypot(a.x - ship.x, a.y - ship.y) < a.r + SHIP_RADIUS * 0.6) {
          ship.alive = false;
          phase = "gameover";
          if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
          beep(110, 0.3);
          break;
        }
      }
    }

    // Wave clear -> spawn next.
    if (asteroids.length === 0 && phase === "playing") {
      spawnWave(5);
    }
  }

  function fire() {
    bullets.push({
      x: ship.x + Math.cos(ship.angle) * SHIP_RADIUS,
      y: ship.y + Math.sin(ship.angle) * SHIP_RADIUS,
      vx: ship.vx + Math.cos(ship.angle) * BULLET_SPEED,
      vy: ship.vy + Math.sin(ship.angle) * BULLET_SPEED,
      life: BULLET_LIFE,
    });
    beep(820, 0.04);
  }

  function splitAsteroid(idx) {
    const a = asteroids[idx];
    asteroids.splice(idx, 1);
    if (a.r === ASTEROID_BIG_R) {
      score += SCORE_BIG;
      for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) addChild(a, ASTEROID_MID_R);
      beep(440, 0.06);
    } else if (a.r === ASTEROID_MID_R) {
      score += SCORE_MID;
      for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) addChild(a, ASTEROID_LIL_R);
      beep(560, 0.05);
    } else {
      score += SCORE_LIL;
      beep(700, 0.05);
    }
  }

  function addChild(parent, r) {
    const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
    const speed = ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0] + Math.random() * (ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[1] - ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0]);
    asteroids.push({
      x: parent.x, y: parent.y,
      vx: Math.cos(angle) * speed,
      vy: Math.sin(angle) * speed,
      r,
      shape: makeAsteroidShape(),
    });
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#05050c";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);

    // Asteroids.
    ctx.strokeStyle = "#a5a5b8";
    ctx.lineWidth = 1.5;
    for (const a of asteroids) drawAsteroid(a);

    // Bullets.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffd23f";
    for (const b of bullets) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(b.x, b.y, 2.4, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
    }

    // Ship.
    if (ship.alive) drawShip();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Best ${highScore}`, W - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "gameover") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(5,5,12,0.65)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText("Game Over", W / 2, H / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, W / 2, H / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", W / 2, H / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  function drawShip() {
    ctx.save();
    ctx.translate(ship.x, ship.y);
    ctx.rotate(ship.angle);
    ctx.strokeStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.lineWidth = 2;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(SHIP_RADIUS, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.9, SHIP_RADIUS * 0.7);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.5, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.9, -SHIP_RADIUS * 0.7);
    ctx.closePath();
    ctx.stroke();
    if (keys && (keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W"))) {
      ctx.strokeStyle = "#ffd23f";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.5, 0);
      ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS - 6 - Math.random() * 4, 0);
      ctx.stroke();
    }
    ctx.restore();
  }

  function drawAsteroid(a) {
    ctx.beginPath();
    for (let i = 0; i < a.shape.length; i++) {
      const t = (i / a.shape.length) * Math.PI * 2;
      const r = a.r * a.shape[i];
      const x = a.x + Math.cos(t) * r;
      const y = a.y + Math.sin(t) * r;
      if (i === 0) ctx.moveTo(x, y); else ctx.lineTo(x, y);
    }
    ctx.closePath();
    ctx.stroke();
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
    if (ev.key === " ") ev.preventDefault();
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "triangle";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.04, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,                                  // "playing" | "gameover"
      score,
      player: {
        x: ship.x,
        y: ship.y,
        // wrap() keeps coords in [0, W) and [0, H), so visible is always true
        // — but assert it explicitly so the contract is honest.
        visible: ship.x >= 0 && ship.x < W && ship.y >= 0 && ship.y < H,
      },
      objective: "Shoot the asteroids before they hit your ship. Big rocks split into smaller ones.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function wrap(v, max) {
    if (v < 0) return v + max;
    if (v >= max) return v - max;
    return v;
  }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

Let's produce index.html.

Will write:

<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Rotator Flip</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #05050c; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Rotator Flip</h1>
    <p class="game-description">The tunnel fills with rotating blade obstacles; tapping instantly reverses their spin direction, letting you align the safe gaps as the dot flies forward.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Tap / click to reverse spin direction</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
    // === CONFIG =============================================================
    const W = 800, H = 600;
    // placeholder config values
    // === STATE ==============================================================
    const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');

    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();

    // initialize player for observable contract
    const player = { x: W/2, y: H/2, visible: true };

    // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ================================================
    function publishState() {
      window.__game = {
        state: 'menu', // could be 'playing' or 'gameover' later
        score: 0,
        player: {
          x: player.x,
          y: player.y,
          visible: player.visible,
        },
        objective: 'Tap / click to reverse spin direction; avoid rotating blades.',
      };
    }

    // === RESET ==============================================================
    function reset() {
      // placeholder reset; just publish state
      publishState();
    }

    // === LOOP ==============================================================
    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          // placeholder update: nothing
          // placeholder render: clear background
          ctx.fillStyle = '#05050c';
          ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
        } catch (e) {
          console.error('loop error', e);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    // === INPUT ==============================================================
    document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
        reset();
      }
    });
    restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);

    // === BOOT ==============================================================
    reset();
    startLoop();
  </script>
</body>
</html>

    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade","reaction","rotator"]
}

Implementation steps:

- Variables: spawnTimer.

- update(dt):
  - if phase === playing:
     - Update player position: player.x += PLAYER_SPEED * dt (forward); also maybe wrap or keep within bounds/ not needed.
     - For each hazard:
        - hazard.x += hazard.vx * dt; hazard.y += hazard.vy * dt;
        - hazard.angle += hazard.spinSpeed * dt; // rotation angle.
    - Remove hazards that moved off-screen (x + r < 0) and increment score (passed hazard).
    - Spawn hazards:
        spawnTimer -= dt;
        if spawnTimer <= 0:
           spawnHazard();
           spawnTimer = spawnInterval; maybe with random interval.

  - Collision detection:
        - For each hazard, compute distance to player. If < hazard.r + PLAYER_RADIUS:
            - Compute relative angle: let dir = Math.atan2(player.y - hazard.y, player.x - hazard.x);
            - Determine angle difference between dir and hazard.angle (gap center). Define GAP_ANGLE = Math.PI / 4 (45 deg). If Math.abs(normalizeAngle(dir - hazard.angle)) > GAP_ANGLE / 2, then collision (i.e., outside gap). If collision, set phase = "gameover"; break.

 - If phase === gameover: maybe stop updating hazards; but still draw.

So script block is:

```
<script>
    // === CONFIG =============================================================
    const W = 800, H = 600;
    // === STATE ==============================================================
    const canvas = document.getElementById('game');
    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d');
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');
    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();
    // placeholder player object for observable contract
    const player = { x: W/2, y: H/2, visible: true };
    // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
    function publishState() {
      window.__game = {
        state: 'menu', // "menu" | "playing" | "gameover"
        score: 0,
        player: {
          x: player.x,
          y: player.y,
          visible: player.visible,
        },
        objective: 'Tap / click to reverse spin direction; avoid rotating blades.',
      };
    }
    // === RESET ==============================================================
    function reset() {
      // Reset any game state here (placeholder)
      publishState();
    }
    // === LOOP ===============================================================
    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          // Placeholder update/render: clear canvas
          ctx.fillStyle = '#05050c';
          ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
        } catch (e) {
          console.error('loop error', e);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    // === INPUT ==============================================================
    document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
      if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
        reset();
      }
    });
    restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);
    // === BOOT ===============================================================
    reset();
    startLoop();
  </script>
```

prompt (5,116 chars)
Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Rotator Flip
Concept: The tunnel fills with rotating blade obstacles; tapping instantly reverses their spin direction, letting you align the safe gaps as the dot flies forward.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.

Stay alive as long as you can.\",\n24:   \"controls\": [\"Arrow keys to move\", \"Space to fire\"],\n25:   \"tags\": [\"arcade\", \"dodge\"]\n26: }\n27: ```\n28: \n29: Rules:\n30: - `title` — string, 3–80 characters, matches the game's display name.\n31: - `description` — string, 20–400 characters, one short paragraph.\n32: - `controls` — non-empty array of short strings, one per control.\n33: - `tags` — optional array of lowercase keywords.\n34: \n35: ## `index.html` requirements\n36: \n37: ### Self-contained\n38: - All CSS goes in a single `<style>` block in `<head>`.\n39: - All JS goes in a single `<script>` block at the end of `<body>`.\n40: - **No** external resources: no `<script src=\"https://...\">`, no `<link href=\"https://...\">`, no `<img src=\"https://...\">`, no CDN imports.\n41: - **No** network calls at runtime: no `fetch`, no `XMLHttpRequest`, no `import()`, no `WebSocket`.\n42: \n43: ### Required structure (in this order)\n44: \n45: ```html\n46: <!DOCTYPE html>\n47: <html lang=\"en\">\n48: <head>\n49:   <meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n50:   <title>YOUR TITLE</title>\n51:   <style>/* inline only */</style>\n52: </head>\n53: <body>\n54:   <header class=\"game-header\">\n55:     <h1>YOUR TITLE</h1>\n56:     <p class=\"game-description\">YOUR DESCRIPTION</p>\n57:     <ul class=\"game-controls\">\n58:       <li>YOUR CONTROL</li>\n59:     </ul>\n60:   </header>\n61:   <main>\n62:     <canvas id=\"game\" width=\"800\" height=\"600\"></canvas>\n63:     <button id=\"restart\" class=\"game-restart\" type=\"button\">Restart</button>\n64:   </main>\n65:   <script>/* inline only */</script>\n66: </body>\n67: </html>\n68: ```\n69: \n70: - Exactly one `<canvas id=\"game\">` (you may set width/height to other values; 800x600 is recommended).\n71: - Exactly one `<button id=\"restart\" class=\"game-restart\">` immediately after the canvas. Clicking it must fully reset the game (same code path as the R key). It must be visible and clickable at all times — not only on game-over.\n72: - The `<header>`'s `<h1>` text must match `meta.json.title` exactly.\n73: - The `<p class=\"game-description\">` text must match `meta.json.description` exactly.\n74: - The `<li>` items in `<ul class=\"game-controls\">` must match `meta.json.controls` in order.\n75: \n76: ### Behavior\n77: - The game must start rendering immediately on page load (no \"click to start\" required).\n78: - Use keyboard and/or mouse input.\n79: - The script must not throw any uncaught exception in the first 5 seconds.\n80: - If you use `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`, all keys must start with the prefix `slop-factory:rotator-flip:`.\n81: \n82: ### Style\n83: - Dark background (the gallery is dark — recommend `#0a0a0f` or similar).\n84: - High-contrast neon accents (e.g. cyan `#7df9ff`, magenta `#ff00aa`, yellow `#ffd23f`).\n85: - The `<header>` is shown above the canvas — keep its styling minimal and let the canvas content shine.\n86: \n87: ## What makes a tiny canvas game feel good\n88: \n89: Aim for these qualities in every game you produce:\n90: \n91: 1. **Immediate input response** — every keystroke or click produces visible action within one frame. Never make the player wait.\n92: 2. **Clear feedback** — the player always knows when something happened. Use color flashes, particles, brief screen shake, score popups, or sound on every meaningful event (hit, score, miss, death).\n93: 3. **Win/lose state** — the game has a clear end condition. On lose, draw \"Game Over\" + final score + restart instructions on the canvas. On win, celebrate (color burst, \"You Win!\" overlay).\n94: 4. **Restart without page reload** — both pressing R AND clicking the `<button id=\"restart\">` reset the game cleanly via the same code path. Mention both in `controls` (e.g. \"Press R or click Restart\"). On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. No external URLs in `<script src=>`, `<link href=>`, `<img src=>` (regex scan).\n190: 5. Headless Chromium loads the page; no `console.error` or `pageerror` events in 5 seconds.\n191: 6. Canvas has non-blank pixels after 3 seconds (game is actually drawing something).\n192: 7. **Observable contract is present**: `window.__game` exists with the exact shape above, from the first frame.\n193: 8. **Playtest invariants** hold during ~8 seconds of synthetic key input (arrow keys, WASD, space):\n194:    - `state` only takes the values `\"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\"`.\n195:    - `player.visible` is true for ≥95% of polled frames.\n196:    - Either `score` changes at least once during play, or `state` reaches `\"gameover\"`.
